{"parentUuid":"976358ee-c1ac-40ef-ba20-31b70f5ac5f9","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a244e081f6ebdab6a","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- heartbeat-protocol: Claude node contract for DebateAI Graph Spine v2. The Main Orchestrator (Claude-Router seat) contract; thin loader over the repo spine. All agent launches use /goal.\n- commit-commands:clean_gone: Cleans up all git branches marked as [gone] (branches that have been deleted on the remote but still exist locally), including removing associated worktrees.\n- commit-commands:commit-push-pr: Commit, push, and open a PR\n- commit-commands:commit: Create a git commit\n- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- mattpocock-skills:diagnosing-bugs: Diagnosis loop for hard bugs and performance regressions. Use when the user says \"diagnose\"/\"debug this\", or reports something broken/throwing/failing/slow.\n- mattpocock-skills:tdd: Test-driven development. Use when the user wants to build features or fix bugs test-first, mentions \"red-green-refactor\", or wants integration tests.\n- mattpocock-skills:prototype: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. Use when the user wants to sanity-check whether a state model or logic feels right, or explore what a UI should look like.\n- mattpocock-skills:research: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent.\n- mattpocock-skills:domain-modeling: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when the user wants to pin down domain terminology or a ubiquitous language, record an architectural decision, or when another skill needs to maintain the domain model.\n- mattpocock-skills:codebase-design: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary.\n- mattpocock-skills:code-review: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to \"review since X\".\n- mattpocock-skills:resolving-merge-conflicts: Use when you need to resolve an in-progress git merge/rebase conflict.\n- mattpocock-skills:grilling: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases.\n- superpowers:brainstorming: You MUST use this before any creative work - creating features, building components, adding functionality, or modifying behavior. Explores user intent, requirements and design before implementation.\n- superpowers:dispatching-parallel-agents: Use when facing 2+ independent tasks that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies\n- superpowers:executing-plans: Use when you have a written implementation plan to execute in a separate session with review checkpoints\n- superpowers:finishing-a-development-branch: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work\n- superpowers:receiving-code-review: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation\n- superpowers:requesting-code-review: Use when completing tasks, implementing major features, or before merging to verify work meets requirements\n- superpowers:subagent-driven-development: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session\n- superpowers:systematic-debugging: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes\n- superpowers:test-driven-development: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code\n- superpowers:using-git-worktrees: Use when starting feature work that needs isolation from current workspace or before executing implementation plans - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback\n- superpowers:using-superpowers: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions\n- superpowers:verification-before-completion: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always\n- superpowers:writing-plans: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code\n- superpowers:writing-skills: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- engineering:architecture: Create or evaluate an architecture decision record (ADR). Use when choosing between technologies (e.g., Kafka vs SQS), documenting a design decision with trade-offs and consequences, reviewing a system design proposal, or designing a new component from requirements and constraints.\n- engineering:code-review: Review code changes for security, performance, and correctness. Trigger with a PR URL or diff, \"review this before I merge\", \"is this code safe?\", or when checking a change for N+1 queries, injection risks, missing edge cases, or error handling gaps.\n- engineering:debug: Structured debugging session — reproduce, isolate, diagnose, and fix. Trigger with an error message or stack trace, \"this works in staging but not prod\", \"something broke after the deploy\", or when behavior diverges from expected and the cause isn't obvious.\n- engineering:deploy-checklist: Pre-deployment verification checklist. Use when about to ship a release, deploying a change with database migrations or feature flags, verifying CI status and approvals before going to production, or documenting rollback triggers ahead of time.\n- engineering:documentation: Write and maintain technical documentation. Trigger with \"write docs for\", \"document this\", \"create a README\", \"write a runbook\", \"onboarding guide\", or when the user needs help with any form of technical writing — API docs, architecture docs, or operational runbooks.\n- engineering:incident-response: Run an incident response workflow — triage, communicate, and write postmortem. Trigger with \"we have an incident\", \"production is down\", an alert that needs severity assessment, a status update mid-incident, or when writing a blameless postmortem after resolution.\n- engineering:standup: Generate a standup update from recent activity. Use when preparing for daily standup, summarizing yesterday's commits and PRs and ticket moves, formatting work into yesterday/today/blockers, or structuring a few rough notes into a shareable update.\n- engineering:system-design: Design systems, services, and architectures. Trigger with \"design a system for\", \"how should we architect\", \"system design for\", \"what's the right architecture for\", or when the user needs help with API design, data modeling, or service boundaries.\n- engineering:tech-debt: Identify, categorize, and prioritize technical debt. Trigger with \"tech debt\", \"technical debt audit\", \"what should we refactor\", \"code health\", or when the user asks about code quality, refactoring priorities, or maintenance backlog.\n- engineering:testing-strategy: Design test strategies and test plans. Trigger with \"how should we test\", \"test strategy for\", \"write tests for\", \"test plan\", \"what tests do we need\", or when the user needs help with testing approaches, coverage, or test architecture.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research: Plan, conduct, and synthesize user research. Trigger with \"user research plan\", \"interview guide\", \"usability test\", \"survey design\", \"research questions\", or when the user needs help with any aspect of understanding their users through research.\n- design:ux-copy: Write or review UX copy — microcopy, error messages, empty states, CTAs. Trigger with \"write copy for\", \"what should this button say?\", \"review this error message\", or when naming a CTA, wording a confirmation dialog, filling an empty state, or writing onboarding text.\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx: Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\n- anthropic-skills:explain-usage: Explain where this session's tokens went, with one simple chart in plain language. Use when the user says things like \"explain my usage\", \"where did my tokens go\", or asks for a usage breakdown.\n- anthropic-skills:morning: Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead.\n- anthropic-skills:pdf: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:pptx: Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email or summary); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user mentions \"deck,\" \"slides,\" \"presentation,\" or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. If a .pptx or .potx file needs to be opened, created, or touched, use this skill.\n- anthropic-skills:schedule: Create or update a scheduled task that runs automatically. Use when the user says things like \"every day\", \"each morning\", \"remind me in an hour\", \"run this at noon\", or wants to reschedule an existing task.\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx: Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-diagramming: Diagramming know-how for Artifacts — when a picture earns its place, how to draw one that shows the real mechanism, and the inline-SVG mechanics that keep it legible in both themes.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact page can be granted — behavior static HTML cannot provide on its own, such as the page reading live or connected data, keeping state shared across viewers, or updating and republishing itself. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: {"parentUuid":"4a824fb8-9fdf-4da8-96cc-00d5737e4577","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a244e081f6ebdab6a","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Cdq8MttuRmdYZ64d1tU8R","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01YW5VRekchsC8nYcD6ndJxy","name":"Bash","input":{"command":"sed -n '44,140p' docs/missions/2026-08-06-v3-programming/design-patterns.md","description":"Read pattern register P1-P8"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":371,"cache_read_input_tokens":109001,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":371,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":16,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011Cdq8MsSbXeczbA8anow8m","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"799f3e93-df62-46fd-90b6-7f3f2247c4ef","timestamp":"2026-08-08T10:31:34.656Z","effort":"xhigh","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}
{"parentUuid":"799f3e93-df62-46fd-90b6-7f3f2247c4ef","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"edddcebe-e909-434d-9cc8-5f05a9e586e7","agentId":"a244e081f6ebdab6a","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YW5VRekchsC8nYcD6ndJxy","type":"tool_result","content":"## The pattern register\n\n**P1 · Modular monolith with enforced boundaries.** One pnpm workspace\n(`RULED — DR-117`), one process family; module law = the dependency-edge table\n(03 §3.1 rows 1–27) — an absent edge is a prohibition. CI asserts the graph;\n`apps/*` are sinks. Never reach across a boundary \"just to read\" code — frozen\nfacts are READ from the DB, rules are CALLED across a declared edge, nothing is\nre-implemented (structural rule 6; AC-85). **A compose service is not a module**:\n`hatchet-engine`, `postgres` and `vllm` run beside the workspace and appear in no\nedge (03 §1.3). [ADR-0001; 03 §3]\n\n**P2 · Functional Core / Imperative Shell.** The scoring arithmetic\n(`packages/propagation`) and the decision function (`battery/decision`) are\npure: no I/O, clock, randomness, DB, model calls — enforced by import fences\n(rules 1/5) + `no-impure-import` lint, not discipline. The fence is airtight\nbecause the closure is small: rows 1 and 4 give those two packages only `kernel`\nand `published-arithmetic`, each itself fenced, so there is no permitted package\nthrough which impurity could arrive. Shells (`apps/runner`, `graph`, `serve`) do\nthe effects. Seam A is the choreography: **materialise → compute → persist** —\nbuild an immutable snapshot, run the pure function, persist the receipt.\n[Seam A; ADR-0004; AC-09/AC-48]\n\n**P3 · Contract-first + single Facade front door.** `packages/contract`\ndeclares every wire shape once; codegen produces client types, runtime\nvalidators, OpenAPI and the AC-61 field inventory from that one source.\n`apps/api` (**Fastify**, `RULED — DR-117`) is the only implementation, **and the\nSSE stream is a route on it, not a second front door**; SSR is an ordinary caller.\nParse, don't validate: inputs cross the boundary only through generated\nvalidators into branded types. **The one-transport law holds through the\nCloudflare proxy** — a buffered stream is fixed as proxy configuration, **never**\nas a second path (ADR-0018 clause 1). [ADR-0002; ADR-0018]\n\n**P4 · Gateway/Adapter for providers (Seam C).** Every model call crosses ONE\ninterface — `call(typed role, lane, CallBound, contractHash, PromptPacket) →\nRawArtifactRef`. Provider implementations are compiled-in adapters selected by\nregister config only (H2a: switching providers is a one-register-row change).\n**`RULED — DR-117`: the local model is one such adapter** — vLLM is a separate\ncompose service reached over HTTP, with no lower bar for anything, and **lineage\nis the SERVED MODEL's maker, never \"vLLM\"** (DR-013's diversity floor, AC-38's\ninventory; ADR-0018 clause 3).\nThe gateway persists the raw artifact UNCONDITIONALLY (parseable or not) and\nwrites the ledger row OUTSIDE any open write transaction — never hold a write\nlock across a model call. **`RULED — DR-115`: the raw artifact is a REAL call's\nartifact.** This gateway is the only place a model artifact enters V3, so it is the\nonly place the no-scaffolded-data law can be broken. A failed call yields a typed\nfailure and a ledger row, **never a substituted artifact**; a cache hit replays a\nrecorded *real* artifact under the contract-hash identity and yields a new ledger\nrow; a runtime path that selects a test double is a BLOCKING finding.\n[Seam C; ADR-0009 clause 1; H1/H2; **DR-115**]\n\n**P5 · Event sourcing with derived status.** Append-only event streams\n(`run_progress_event`, `run_row_activation_event`, `served_number_event`,\n`segment_suppression`) are the only mutation; current state = fold(events),\ncomputed on read, never stored. Initial events are written in the same\ntransaction as the frozen head; an empty stream is a TYPED ERROR on read,\nnever a default. If a fact is derivable, deriving it is mandatory (02 §14's\n13 derived-only rows). [ADR-0006/0007; AC-88]\n\n**P6 · CQRS-lite: sealed artifacts vs read-time projections (Seam D).** The\nwrite side seals frozen artifacts (fact bundle, conformance record, served\nnumbers + provenance); the read side computes projections (badges, marks,\nsummaries, current staleness) at read time from typed fields. `answer_index`\nis a VIEW, never a materialized cache. `?version=` returns sealed artifacts;\nno version param returns latest + current projection — reads legitimately\ndiffer, so tests pin the version. [Seam D; ADR-0007/0014]\n\n**P7 · Aggregate with a single-writer transaction (Seam B).** All node/arrow\nwrites go through `graph`'s write API (`withGraphWrite` + `GraphWriter`) in\none transaction under a per-graph advisory lock. Invariants live at the\naggregate boundary: cycle refusal-and-redirect (layer 1), write-time recursive\nreachability (layer 2), pure-core typed error (layer 3) — all three exist.\n[Seam B; ADR-0005; 03 §8]\n\n**P8 · Strategy + Registry via resolution chains.** Every configurable\nbehaviour (operators accumulate/strict-and first among them) sits behind a\nstrategy interface; selection resolves parent → run → deployment through the\nregister, the supplying level is RECORDED on the produced number, and the\ndeployment row is mandatory (DR-074). A source-literal selection is a defect\nby definition. No silent defaults: `Unresolved` blocks visibly, the loader\nsubstitutes nothing. [ADR-0011; DR-074; H4; FX-PT-D2]\n\n**P9 · Pipeline of typed gates.** Serve is a fixed machine-ordered gate chain\n— R9 → Q53 → conformance → Q51, plus the DR-082/086 band-cap — where every\ngate returns a member of a closed outcome vocabulary and every terminal is a\nVALUE (components-only + DEFECT, downgrade, block), never an exception.\n`max_recompose = 2`; three compose-time routes to components-only; post-serve\neviction is a degradation, not a fourth route. [DR-049/057; DR-082/086; AC-52/53]\n\n**P10 · Append-only ledger + content-addressed identity.** Total order from a\ndedicated sequence allocator under a write lock (never timestamps). Named hash\ncolumns each hash one thing (`input_hash` excludes the contract hash;\n`contract_hash` is in cache identity); a cache hit yields a NEW row.\nImmutability is a database property: UPDATE and DELETE revoked + raising\ntriggers. [ADR-0006]\n\n**P11 · Transactional work queue behind a dispatcher (competing consumers +\nidempotent commands).** `core.work_item` claimed via","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"20696536-5f6d-4575-a646-787eb31806d1","timestamp":"2026-08-08T10:31:34.675Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"799f3e93-df62-46fd-90b6-7f3f2247c4ef","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

So the real question is whether the ticket's requirements actually demanded the panel be attached and operational, or if the current state satisfies the \"all fire\" criterion since the unit test fixtures do fire and the weight selection rule is declared with provenance.\n\nBut there's a standing expectation from the S03 diamond note: unattached seams must be honestly declared in the generated audit report, not just mentioned in prose or left out entirely. The handoff violates this by omitting any disclosure at all, which repeats the exact debt class the orchestrator flagged. The reviewer instruction asks to verify seam attachment and truthfulness—this one is truthful about the single judgement, but the new unattached S04 surface with no honesty row in the audit is misleading, especially given the claim of \"real attachment\" without qualification. That feels blocking for honesty since it's a cheap fix: add the orphan rows and assertion. Though I should double-check whether runJudgePanel and related exports are truly never called, or if there's a path I'm missing.\n\nActually, looking closer: the runner has only one provider gateway with one model configured, and a real panel needs at least two members with different actors. So attaching a genuine panel in S04 would require multi-provider setup, which falls outside the ruled inputs since register rows are constrained at VG-02. An honest deferral row is the right move, but it's missing. That makes this a judgment call between blocking and non-blocking. Given S03 was explicit about landing the attachment or deferring with an honesty row in the audit report, and this successor ticket added a larger unattached surface without that declaration, I'm ranking it blocking—the audit report is the project's honesty artifact and it now materially understates the never-called surface.\n\nNow I need to verify a few other things: first, whether the runner's `judgementPolicy` requirement is satisfied in main.ts, since DR-115 flags hardcoded values as violations. The handoff notes that production main supplies none and fails loudly. I should check the actual main.ts and register loader to confirm. I'm also looking at the fence regex for `parseStructuredArtifact`—it handles one fence correctly. Then there's a potential issue in `Judge.judge` where `classifyContent` is called and if parsing succeeds the artifact gets marked as PARSED, but I need to trace through what happens after that call.\n\nThere's also a DR-115-adjacent concern: when the model declares \"RAN\" as the way of knowing but the code silently relabels it to \"REASONING\" based on whether it's pinned, that's a lossy coercion and a mislabel of provenance. The existing S00 test shows this relabeling behavior for unpinned LOOKED_UP, but \"RAN\" → \"REASONING\" feels like it's silently downgrading what the model actually said. I need to check if the `way_of_knowing` enum includes RAN and understand the implications. There's a test in the S00 suite that confirms this behavior — \"relabels unpinned LOOKED_UP as REASONING\" — so it's definitely pre-existing. The `reduceAssessment` function applies caps in their declared order, only when the score exceeds each cap's threshold, which aligns with the ticket's requirement to record the ordering and reasoning. The uncertainty ladder lookup also depends on declaration order, so if the rows aren't properly sorted, the label could be wrong.\n\nThe `measureDispersion` function counts distinct judgement references to determine distinctness, but then spreads the calculation across all entries including duplicates, which is fine. The `applyCorrelatedErrorDiscount` function spreads the judgement object in its return, which could theoretically leak provider or model strings if the caller passes a familyRef containing them, but that's the caller's responsibility — the test already validates against this. {"parentUuid":"f286dbe9-cbe0-4d50-9387-62ad438772ce","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"edddcebe-e909-434d-9cc8-5f05a9e586e7","agentId":"a244e081f6ebdab6a","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01C7YhKMpWdanQEGFRwmqYMS","type":"tool_result","content":"import { readFile } from \"node:fs/promises\";\nimport type { Pool } from \"pg\";\nimport { z } from \"zod\";\nimport {\n  OPERATOR_SUPPLYING_LEVELS,\n  SCORING_OPERATORS,\n  type OperatorSupplyingLevel,\n  type ScoringOperator\n} from \"@debateai/kernel\";\n\nconst bootstrapKeys = [\n  \"nodeRuntimeVersion\",\n  \"pnpmVersion\",\n  \"postgresMajorVersion\",\n  \"typescriptVersion\",\n  \"vllmImageDigest\"\n] as const;\n\nexport type BootstrapKey = typeof bootstrapKeys[number];\n\nconst bootstrapSchema = z.object({\n  registerVersion: z.literal(1),\n  values: z.object({\n    nodeRuntimeVersion: z.string().min(1),\n    pnpmVersion: z.string().min(1),\n    postgresMajorVersion: z.string().regex(/^\\d+$/),\n    typescriptVersion: z.string().min(1),\n    vllmImageDigest: z.string().regex(/^sha256:[a-f0-9]{64}$/)\n  }).strict(),\n  resolution: z.record(z.enum(bootstrapKeys), z.string().min(1))\n});\n\nexport type BootstrapRegister = z.infer<typeof bootstrapSchema>;\n\nexport async function loadBootstrapRegister(\n  location = new URL(\"../../../register.bootstrap.json\", import.meta.url)\n): Promise<BootstrapRegister> {\n  const raw = await readFile(location, \"utf8\");\n  return bootstrapSchema.parse(JSON.parse(raw));\n}\n\nexport type RegisterLevel = OperatorSupplyingLevel;\n\nexport function resolveRegisterValue<T>(\n  key: string,\n  levels: Readonly<Record<RegisterLevel, Readonly<Record<string, T>>>>\n): { readonly value: T; readonly suppliedBy: RegisterLevel } {\n  for (const suppliedBy of OPERATOR_SUPPLYING_LEVELS) {\n    if (Object.hasOwn(levels[suppliedBy], key)) {\n      return { value: levels[suppliedBy][key]!, suppliedBy };\n    }\n  }\n  throw new Error(`Unresolved register key: ${key}`);\n}\n\nexport function resolveScoringOperator(\n  levels: Readonly<Record<RegisterLevel, Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>>>\n): { readonly value: ScoringOperator; readonly suppliedBy: RegisterLevel } {\n  if (!Object.hasOwn(levels.deployment, \"scoringOperator\")) {\n    throw new Error(\"Mandatory deployment register row is missing: scoringOperator\");\n  }\n  for (const suppliedBy of OPERATOR_SUPPLYING_LEVELS) {\n    if (!Object.hasOwn(levels[suppliedBy], \"scoringOperator\")) continue;\n    const value = levels[suppliedBy].scoringOperator;\n    if (!(SCORING_OPERATORS as readonly unknown[]).includes(value)) {\n      throw new Error(`Invalid scoringOperator register value at ${suppliedBy}`);\n    }\n    return Object.freeze({ value: value as ScoringOperator, suppliedBy });\n  }\n  throw new Error(\"Mandatory deployment register row is missing: scoringOperator\");\n}\n\nexport async function persistBootstrapRegister(pool: Pool, bootstrap: BootstrapRegister): Promise<void> {\n  const client = await pool.connect();\n  try {\n    await client.query(\"BEGIN\");\n    for (const key of bootstrapKeys) {\n      await client.query(\n        `INSERT INTO register.register_row (register_version, row_key, value_json, source_ref)\n         VALUES ($1, $2, $3::jsonb, $4)\n         ON CONFLICT (register_version, row_key) DO NOTHING`,\n        [bootstrap.registerVersion, key, JSON.stringify(bootstrap.values[key]), bootstrap.resolution[key]]\n      );\n    }\n    await client.query(\n      `INSERT INTO register.register_version (register_version, row_count, sealed)\n       VALUES ($1, $2, true)\n       ON CONFLICT (register_version) DO NOTHING`,\n      [bootstrap.registerVersion, bootstrapKeys.length]\n    );\n    await client.query(\"COMMIT\");\n  } catch (error) {\n    await client.query(\"ROLLBACK\");\n    throw error;\n  } finally {\n    client.release();\n  }\n}\n\nexport async function assertBootstrapEquality(pool: Pool, bootstrap: BootstrapRegister): Promise<void> {\n  const result = await pool.query<{ row_key: BootstrapKey; value_json: unknown }>(\n    `SELECT row_key, value_json FROM register.register_row\n     WHERE register_version = $1 AND row_key = ANY($2::text[])\n     ORDER BY row_key`,\n    [bootstrap.registerVersion, bootstrapKeys]\n  );\n  if (result.rows.length !== bootstrapKeys.length) {\n    throw new Error(`FX-REG-01 bootstrap row count mismatch: expected ${bootstrapKeys.length}, received ${result.rows.length}`);\n  }\n  for (const row of result.rows) {\n    if (row.value_json !== bootstrap.values[row.row_key]) {\n      throw new Error(`FX-REG-01 bootstrap mismatch at ${row.row_key}`);\n    }\n  }\n}\n\nexport {\n  loadApiEnvironment,\n  loadMigrationEnvironment,\n  loadReplaySelfTestEnvironment,\n=== bootstrap keys ===\n<class 'dict'>\n{\n \"registerVersion\": 1,\n \"values\": {\n  \"nodeRuntimeVersion\": \"v22.23.1\",\n  \"pnpmVersion\": \"11.20.0\",\n  \"postgresMajorVersion\": \"18\",\n  \"typescriptVersion\": \"7.0.2\",\n  \"vllmImageDigest\": \"sha256:ffb2d59b1c059a5bd8d781320c9f5189de8293693b7d95da54befddaa54abf52\"\n },\n \"resolution\": {\n  \"nodeRuntimeVersion\": \"node --version on 2026-08-07\",\n  \"pnpmVersion\": \"pnpm --version on 2026-08-07\",\n  \"postgresMajorVersion\": \"embedded-postgres darwin-arm64 postgres --version on 2026-08-07\",\n  \"typescriptVersion\": \"pnpm exec tsc --version on 2026-08-07\",\n  \"vllmImageDigest\": \"Docker Hub registry API manifest HEAD for vllm/vllm-openai:latest on 2026-08-07\"\n }\n}","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"92d92d94-ffa0-43af-b33d-ed5be4e115e6","timestamp":"2026-08-08T10:33:08.804Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f286dbe9-cbe0-4d50-9387-62ad438772ce","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

{"parentUuid":"18a8c482-6db0-4661-85c1-6747519a853f","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c4300b0e-2187-4404-89d5-5d17861c16f8","agentId":"a244e081f6ebdab6a","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015yPHLc4c1Ev4KFr1doZRRs","type":"tool_result","content":"    await persistBootstrapRegister(database.pool, bootstrap);\n    await assertBootstrapEquality(database.pool, bootstrap);\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"apps/runner — legal command lifecycle\", () => {\n  it(\"claims, judges through the HTTP gateway, propagates, serves, and settles\", async () => {\n    const provider = await startProviderDouble([\n      JSON.stringify({ statement: \"A provisional answer.\", way_of_knowing: \"REASONING\",\n        locator: null, restatement_text: \"A provisional answer.\", restatement_status: \"PASS\", value_laden: false,\n        steelman: { summary: \"A provisional answer.\", fidelity: 0.72 }, critic: { summary: \"Plausible counter.\", counterargumentStrength: 0.28, basis: \"PLAUSIBLE_COUNTER\" },\n        evidence: { quality: 0.72, relevance: 0.72 }, context: { fit: 0.72, ambiguityFlags: [] }, fallacy: { severity: 0.28, fatalFlags: [] } }),\n      JSON.stringify({ segments: [\"A provisional answer.\", \"Check an independent source.\"] }),\n      JSON.stringify({ conforms: true, findings: [] }),\n      JSON.stringify({ conforms: true, findings: [] }),\n      JSON.stringify({ pass: true })\n    ]);\n    try {\n      const work = await createRunnerWork(\"happy-path\");\n      const result = await runnerWithEndpoint(provider.endpoint).executeWorkItem(work.workItemId);\n      expect(result.kind).toBe(\"COMPLETED\");\n      expect(provider.calls()).toBe(5);\n      const row = await database.pool.query<{ state: string; terminal: string; base_kind: string; final_kind: string; base_producer: string; final_producer: string }>(\n        `SELECT work.state, answer.terminal, judgement.number_kind AS base_kind, strength.number_kind AS final_kind,\n                judgement.producer AS base_producer, strength.producer AS final_producer\n         FROM core.work_item AS work JOIN serve.answer AS answer ON answer.answer_id = work.settled_artifact_ref\n         JOIN core.node AS node ON node.run_id = work.run_id\n         JOIN ledger.reduced_judgement AS judgement ON judgement.node_id = node.node_id\n         JOIN ledger.node_strength_record AS strength ON strength.node_id = node.node_id\n         WHERE work.work_item_id = $1`, [work.workItemId]\n      );\n      expect(row.rows[0]).toMatchObject({ state: \"DONE\", terminal: \"DOWNGRADED\",\n        base_kind: \"base-probability\", final_kind: \"propagated-probability\",\n        base_producer: \"judgement:test-layer\", final_producer: \"propagation:test-layer\" });\n      if (result.kind !== \"COMPLETED\") throw new Error(\"TEST_EXPECTED_COMPLETION\");\n      const projection = await new ServeRepository(database.pool).readAnswerProjection(result.answerId, \"asker:happy-path\");\n      expect(projection?.nodes[0]?.base_score).toMatchObject({\n        kind: \"base-probability\", producer: \"judgement:test-layer\"\n      });\n      expect(projection?.nodes[0]?.base_score.replay_handle).toMatch(/^judgement:/);\n      expect(projection?.nodes[0]?.final_strength).toMatchObject({\n        kind: \"propagated-probability\", producer: \"propagation:test-layer\"\n      });\n    } finally { await provider.stop(); }\n  });\n\n  it(\"persists and settles a pre-compose BLOCK terminal as a value\", async () => {\n    const provider = await startProviderDouble([JSON.stringify({\n      statement: \"A blocked answer.\", way_of_knowing: \"REASONING\", locator: null,\n      restatement_text: \"Different meaning.\", restatement_status: \"FAIL\", value_laden: false,\n      steelman: { summary: \"A blocked answer.\", fidelity: 0.4 }, critic: { summary: \"Plausible counter.\", counterargumentStrength: 0.6, basis: \"PLAUSIBLE_COUNTER\" },\n      evidence: { quality: 0.4, relevance: 0.4 }, context: { fit: 0.4, ambiguityFlags: [] }, fallacy: { severity: 0.6, fatalFlags: [] }\n    })]);\n    try {\n      const work = await createRunnerWork(\"pre-compose-block\");\n      const result = await runnerWithEndpoint(provider.endpoint).executeWorkItem(work.workItemId);\n      expect(result.kind).toBe(\"COMPLETED\");\n      const row = await database.pool.query<{ state: string; terminal: string; serve_state: string; composed_text_id: string | null }>(\n        `SELECT work.state, answer.terminal, answer.serve_state, answer.composed_text_id\n         FROM core.work_item AS work JOIN serve.answer AS answer ON answer.answer_id = work.settled_artifact_ref\n         WHERE work.work_item_id = $1`, [work.workItemId]\n      );\n      expect(row.rows[0]).toEqual({ state: \"DONE\", terminal: \"BLOCKED\", serve_state: \"BLOCKED\", composed_text_id: null });\n      if (result.kind !== \"COMPLETED\") throw new Error(\"TEST_EXPECTED_COMPLETION\");\n      const projection = await new ServeRepository(database.pool).readAnswerProjection(result.answerId, \"asker:pre-compose-block\");\n      expect(projection?.conformance_outcome).toBe(\"NOT_RUN\");","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"0072e6e6-71a6-4d10-931a-f0847f418e29","timestamp":"2026-08-08T10:34:07.161Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"18a8c482-6db0-4661-85c1-6747519a853f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}
{"parentUuid":"0072e6e6-71a6-4d10-931a-f0847f418e29","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a244e081f6ebdab6a","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Cdq8ZMEynLNV1fbTaXLNW","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"The runner DB test validates the recordReduced insert against actual PostgreSQL and confirms the constraint is satisfied, but it's not verifying the values of the new S04 columns like uncertainty_ladder_position, score_caps, holes, branch_identifier, selected_judgement_ref, panel_contract_hashes, disagreement, dispersion, and claim_type on the core node, or parse_error on raw_artifact, or judgement_selection_rule_key.\n\nThe handoff explicitly asked me to verify migration 0004_s04.sql, recordReduced, the new graph claim_type write, raw parse-error persistence, and the runner's selection-rule provenance columns on real PostgreSQL — but none of those values are actually being asserted. This is a real TDD gap: the DDL CHECK constraint for parse_error pairing (PARSE_FAILED/SCHEMA_FAILED requires non-blank parse_error) is never exercised on a real database, which is a genuine risk. I traced through the gateway's classifyContent logic — for PARSE_FAILURE it returns a non-blank message from parseStructuredArtifact, and for SCHEMA_FAILURE it returns a zod error message, both non-blank. The contentParseStatus default path returns PARSED/UNPARSED with null parseError, which is fine. But there's a real bug risk lurking somewhere.\n\nNow I'm actively hunting for a defect by reasoning through the DDL constraint interaction. The raw_artifact_parse_error_pair CHECK enforces that either parse_status is PARSED/UNPARSED with null parse_error, or it's PARSE_FAILED/SCHEMA_FAILED with non-blank parse_error. If parse_status itself is NULL, the CHECK evaluates to NULL and passes — that's fine. I'm tracing through the OpenAICompatibleProviderGateway.call path: when the provider returns a non-OK status or malformed response, content is null, so parseStatus becomes UNPARSED with null parseError, which satisfies the constraint. But when classifyContent throws an exception, I need to see how that's handled.\n\nLooking at the judge path now — classifyContent parses the content with judgeArtifactSchema, and then Judge.judge parses response.content again with the same schema. Since response.content is the same string from the API response, that's consistent. But I'm noticing something in the DR-115 sweep: in Judge.judge, wayOfKnowing gets set to either \"LOOKED_UP\" or \"REASONING\", and RAN silently becomes REASONING — that's a data synthesis point worth examining.\n\nThen in reduceAssessment, tau is computed from declared coefficients rather than fabricated. And in runJudgePanel, the primary judge is always included even if its assessment is already provided. But there's an issue with AC-92 — the primary carries a memberRole but has a null contractHash, so the panel record can't actually attest the primary's contract. The panelContractHashes ends up hardcoded as just the judge's contract hash rather than being derived from the full panel.\n\nThe dispersion measurement also has a gap: when there are fewer than two parseable judgements, measureDispersion returns ABSENT, but the runner writes dispersion as null to the database with a NOT_MEASURED reason in the disagreement JSON. So a null dispersion column effectively means typed-absent.\n\nHowever, there's a data-model issue here — the disagreement column is carrying a dispersion-specific reason, which conflates two separate concepts: the disagreement flag (FX-S22-01) and the dispersion measurement (FX-LG-15). Meanwhile, applyDeclaredDisagreement returns either \"DISAGREEMENT\" or \"NO_DISAGREEMENT\" as the A reducer\n1028:| **`reduced_judgement_ref`** | the `reduced_judgement` row (§6.3) whose selected judgement produced this τ | DR-077's selection is the join between the panel and the served number; without the ref, `strength` and `selected_judgement_ref` sit in two tables with no key between them and the ceremony cannot walk from a served number to the judgement that became it | **DR-077** |\n1030:**No value is duplicated.** `dispersion` stays on `reduced_judgement` and the\n2063:| the node's **live lifecycle surface** — *generating → being judged → scored* | **never persisted, in all three positions**: *generating* as above; *being judged* from a `JUDGEMENT_SCHEDULED` ledger row (§11A.4) with no `reduced_judgement` yet; *scored* from the presence of a `node_strength_record` row for that node in the run's latest propagation run | **DR-076**; AC-88 |\n2199:| Q-09 (U-4 ≡ A-6) — what judge weight multiplies | **DR-077** | the weight is consumed in **selection** under a declared rule, never by averaging; dispersion is measured separately and typed absent below two judgements (§6.3, §6.4a, §6.5) |\n2287:DR-076 (lifecycle derived-only), DR-077 (selection + dispersion), DR-078","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"6db87e6c-582e-4d9b-a5ce-ce0c10db166e","timestamp":"2026-08-08T10:34:36.534Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"93bf3149-1eea-4c6d-a86a-3c9fffd61e13","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

{"parentUuid":"7b0825f1-0695-4743-80fe-3d1a28fb14df","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c4300b0e-2187-4404-89d5-5d17861c16f8","agentId":"a244e081f6ebdab6a","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TqCUCMVrunW4soPBMD6qaA","type":"tool_result","content":"### 6.3 `reduced_judgement`\n\nThe deterministic reducer's output: τ, uncertainty-ladder position, **drivers in\nfixed emission order**, **ordered score caps with what/to-what/why/by-what**,\ntyped holes, **branch identifier**, `reducer_version`, and\n**`judge_weight_version`**.\n\nThe last is not optional: DR-034 plus DR-060(b) require every served number to\nrecompute **byte-identically** from frozen records with no model in the path, so\n**any input that moved a served number — including the judge weight in force and\nits version — is a frozen replay input** (Plan.md §6.3 U-5, RESOLVED-BY-PACK by\nentailment).\n\n**What the weight multiplies is ruled at DR-077, and the outcome is recorded\nhere.** A judge's earned weight **multiplies the served arithmetic** — consumed\nin the **selection** of which judgement becomes the reduced score, under a\n**declared rule, never by averaging** — and **dispersion is measured and served\nseparately, never blended away**. Two columns carry the ruled outcome, and\nwithout them the ceremony can read the τ and not what produced it:\n\n| Column | Carries | Constraint |\n|---|---|---|\n| **`selected_judgement_ref`** | **which** judgement the declared rule selected, out of the candidate set the panel produced | DR-077; AC-34 (the number joined to its origin) |\n| **`dispersion`** | the measured dispersion across the panel — **a separate served quantity, never folded into τ**; **typed absent, never zero, below two judgements** (P15; AC-04's bulkhead) | DR-077; AC-66 |\n\n**The rule's identity lives once, on `propagation_run`** (§6.4) — it resolves\nfrom the register like the operator does, so recording it per judgement would be\na second copy of a per-run fact (AC-85). This row records **what the rule\nselected**; that row records **which rule was in force and which level supplied\nit**.\n\n**Averaging is unrepresentable rather than forbidden by convention**: there is no\ncolumn for a blended τ and no column for a dispersion-adjusted one. A reducer\nthat averaged would have to write the average into `tau` and leave\n`selected_judgement_ref` pointing at a judgement whose τ differs from it — which\nthe S4 fixture checks directly.\n\n### 6.4 `propagation_run` — the evaluation snapshot's receipt\n\nCarries: the **graph fingerprint** (input-order-independent; changes when any τ\nchanges; **differs between two operator selections**), **the recorded arrow\norder**, the per-parent **operator identifier and the resolution level that\nsupplied it** (AC-22), the **cluster records** (AC-23), engine version — **plus\nthe five ruled structural outcomes below**.\n\n#### 6.4a The recorded-outcome columns — one per ruled structural outcome\n\n**The rule this section exists to satisfy.** DR-034/AC-07 make the replay\nceremony read **frozen rows as data**, and `apps/replay` may recompute nothing\nV3-specific (`03-module-design.md` §3.3, §5.5). So **a ruled structural outcome\nthat is not a recorded column is an outcome the ceremony cannot check** — the\nceremony would pass while saying nothing about it. Every ruling of Theme C\ntherefore lands as a column, here or on `node_strength_record` (§6.5):\n\n| Ruled outcome | Column | Grain | Ruling |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **transmission reductions** — the reduction each undercut applied to its targeted support edge's transmitted contribution | **`transmission_reductions`** — `{target_edge_id, undercut_edge_id, reduction, magnitude_status}` | **per edge** (DR-071's own words) | **DR-071** |\n| **lift markers** — which lift applied, to what, in what order, with the marker at **both** ends | **`lift_records`** — `{node_id, lift_kind ∈ {FOLDER, JUDGED_ANCESTOR}, lift_target_node_id, marker_at_source, marker_at_target, applied_ordinal}`; projected per node at §6.5's `lift_marker` | per node, ordered | **DR-072** |\n| **cluster records, both polarities** | **`cluster_records`** — `{cluster_id, key, key_basis, polarity ∈ {support, attack}, absorbed_edge_ids, surviving_member}` | per cluster | **DR-073** |\n| **effective operator + supplying level** | **`operator_by_parent`** — `{parent_node_id, operator, supplying_level}`; mirrored per node at §6.5 | per parent | **DR-074** (AC-22's chain) |","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"f9d4366c-656b-4c86-bb82-ed241c22e8c1","timestamp":"2026-08-08T10:34:38.635Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7b0825f1-0695-4743-80fe-3d1a28fb14df","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}

{"parentUuid":"caba3d61-8e61-411f-9e0b-b6b656cac028","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"c4300b0e-2187-4404-89d5-5d17861c16f8","agentId":"a244e081f6ebdab6a","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YHgWkcMbm1KmFp7DcZjmKu","type":"tool_result","content":"  storage-engine-specific ordering manifest §8.2g refuses to carry — AC-04);\n- because the order is **recorded**, **every recomputation of an\n  already-computed run consumes the recorded order and never re-derives it** —\n  which binds the overlay-detachment byte-identity check (AC-30), the replay\n  ceremony (AC-07) and the removal-based leverage/fragility recomputations\n  (AC-29).\n\nA property test asserts the derived order is **stable across two independent\nderivations of the same snapshot** (AC-08; Plan.md §3.2, §8 S2).\n\n**Cluster records** are emitted by the compute-time collapse gate **inside the\npure core** and recorded here — stored arrow strengths remain the outputs of\ntheir ruled producers (AC-27), the record is itself a frozen replay input and an\ninput to the graph fingerprint, and the key is **printed wherever a cluster\nchanged a number** (AC-23; Plan.md §6.4 A-5). **DR-073 rules that collapse\napplies to BOTH polarities**, support and attack alike, which is why `polarity`\nis a column of the record (§6.4a) rather than an assumption about it.\n\n### 6.5 `node_strength_record`\n\n**One row per node per propagation run**:\n`{strength, tau_source, way_of_knowing, cluster_id, judged_by, abstained,\nsupported_by, attacked_by, operator_used, position_label, lift_marker}`\n(manifest §4.3) — **never a flat map** (AC-34). The debug facet reads this same\nper-node record, not a parallel one (AC-34; manifest §9.3).\n\n**Three additions the Theme C rulings force**, each the per-node half of a §6.4a\noutcome:\n\n| Column | Carries | Why it is here and not only at §6.4 | Ruling |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **`operator_level`** | the resolution level that supplied `operator_used` — parent, run or deployment | `operator_used` alone records *what* without *where from*, and DR-074's whole anti-defect property is that the operator is **a recorded config value, never a hardcoded literal**. A record naming the operator and not its supplying level cannot tell a register-resolved value from a source literal, which is exactly D2 | **DR-074** |\n| **`lift_marker`** *(shape fixed, not added)* | `{lift_kind, lift_target_node_id, marker_at_source, marker_at_target, applied_ordinal}` — the per-node projection of §6.4a's `lift_records`, **never a bare boolean** | manifest §4.3 names the field; DR-072 gives it content. A marker that says *\"a lift happened\"* and not *which* one or *to what* leaves both ends of DR-072's ruled marker pair unrecorded | **DR-072** |\n| **`reduced_judgement_ref`** | the `reduced_judgement` row (§6.3) whose selected judgement produced this τ | DR-077's selection is the join between the panel and the served number; without the ref, `strength` and `selected_judgement_ref` sit in two tables with no key between them and the ceremony cannot walk from a served number to the judgement that became it | **DR-077** |\n\n**No value is duplicated.** `dispersion` stays on `reduced_judgement` and the\nselection **rule** stays on `propagation_run`; this row carries the **level**,\nthe **markers** and the **ref** — three facts that are per node per run and are\nrecorded nowhere else (AC-85).\n\n### 6.6 `decision_record`\n\nThe pure decision function's inputs, firing reasons, `categorical|scalar`\nclassification, **blockers (recorded, excluded from classification)**, spawn\ncount, and a **replay identity hash excluding the idempotency key, spawn count\nand classification fields** (AC-48; manifest §7.2a–f).\n\n### 6.7 Partitioning\n\n`ledger_entry` and `raw_artifact` are **partitioned by run range**, and **no\npartition is ever dropped** (AC-05; Plan.md §4.3). Partitioning is a physical","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"999366a5-2dba-4717-b2ea-e6affba24fba","timestamp":"2026-08-08T10:34:54.259Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"caba3d61-8e61-411f-9e0b-b6b656cac028","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/vladmihaimiron/Documents/DebateAI-V3","sessionId":"a8fcf2e3-bdd1-4a10-bcd6-3ec887012907","version":"2.1.221","gitBranch":"main","slug":"hidden-riding-bengio"}
